test source is uncommitted at that head. @2026-11-06T20:51:53.022Z"}],"checkpointProseSnapshot":"## Did\nRoot cause: After= only ordered tunnel startup; WantedBy=default.target let the tunnel run independently of the failed VM service. Restart=always, RestartSec=15, and StartLimitIntervalSec=0 retried SSH to 172.31.44.2 every ~15 seconds; the kernel returned No route to host and ssh exited 255. Added BindsTo=papercusp-vm-mac.service and moved WantedBy to papercusp-vm-mac.service. Updated the README and regression test; the installed tunnel is inactive while the VM service is failed. Test row 23509022 passed 4/4.\n\n## Left\nThe code/config fix is verified, but EI-27981905198188748 is still open. Main build d740fc rejects the current multi-kind completion proof. Two staging ptool write attempts were blocked before dispatch because the plan-execution authority lookup to :9071 timed out at 1500ms; no close mutation was written. Existing WI-6740 is done and concerns proxy upstream timeouts; whether it covers this authority-lookup timeout is unknown. VM remains failed and was not started.\n\n## Key insight\nAfter= does not bind lifetimes; BindsTo plus WantedBy ties the tunnel to the VM service. The completion must use staging because main d740fc lacks the current kindEvidence schema. Proxy logs show queued critical-continuation requests and socket resets, but no direct cause for the authority-fetch timeout is established.\n\n## Next action\nOn the next cold wake, reorient and resume this item. Check the current :9071 authority path, then validate and submit the staging work_items:complete call with workOutcome=introduced-change. Do not use the older main close schema; check whether the timeout is covered by WI-6740 before filing a duplicate.\n\n---\n## Did\nRoot cause: After= only ordered tunnel startup; WantedBy=default.target let the tunnel run independently of the failed VM service. Restart=always, RestartSec=15, and StartLimitIntervalSec=0 then retried SSH to 172.31.44.2 every ~15 seconds; the kernel returned No route to host and ssh exited 255. Added BindsTo=papercusp-vm-mac.service and moved WantedBy to papercusp-vm-mac.service. Updated the README and added a focused regression test. Installed the unit, reloaded systemd, stopped the independent retry loop, and enabled the tunnel under the VM service.\n\n## Left\nNo implementation residue remains in this item. The VM service itself remains failed and the inference route remains unavailable; I left the VM stopped, consistent with the existing macOS release plan.\n\n## Key insight\nAfter= only orders startup; it does not bind lifetimes. BindsTo plus WantedBy makes the VM service own the tunnel while preserving SSH retries when the VM service is active.\n\n## Next action\nClose this item with root-cause evidence and config, test-only, and docs proofs; then refresh the cold-loop carry and end for the next 60-second wake.\n\n---\n## Did\nConfirmed the failure chain: papercusp-vm-mac.service is failed, while the tunnel was independently enabled under default.target with Restart=always, RestartSec=15 and StartLimitIntervalSec=0. Recent tunnel journal entries show ssh to 172.31.44.2:22 returning No route to host and exiting 255. Added BindsTo=papercusp-vm-mac.service and moved WantedBy to papercusp-vm-mac.service; updated the README and a focused unit regression test. Installed the source unit, reloaded systemd, stopped the independent retry loop, and enabled the tunnel under the VM service.\n\n## Left\nThe VM service remains failed and the inference route remains unavailable. I did not start or repair the VM; the existing plan records it was intentionally left stopped pending VM launcher recovery. The tunnel is now enabled under the VM service and inactive while that service is failed.\n\n## Key insight\nAfter= only orders unit startup; it does not bind lifetimes. BindsTo plus WantedBy makes the VM service own the tunnel while preserving retries for guest SSH readiness when the VM service is active.\n\n## Next action\nClose this item with the recorded root-cause evidence and config, test-only, and docs proofs. Current phase/queue tests and installed-socket integration pass.","distinguishingTest":"Correlate the exact requestId in the client log with its progress state and 30s timeout, then query the service journal for the same incident window.","alternativeHypothesis":"The client failed before the service accepted the request, so the fallback came from a socket/connect/protocol failure rather than queued service work.","predictedObservations":{"hypothesis":"The same requestId appears in a queued progress response before the 30000ms timeout; the old service may have no phase trace.","alternativeHypothesis":"No queued progress for that requestId appears before a connection/protocol error or timeout."}},"checkpointProseSnapshot":"## Did\nFor EI-27984456739598051, the historical client log for request ee58ed89-1bfc-4d97-bdb2-aaf86476f73f contains one `queued` progress response, then `timed-out` at 30000ms with full-compile fallback. The service journal has no matching entries (nor any entries in 2026-11-05 16:10–16:14 local), so the exact internal phase of that old request is unrecoverable. Current staging source emits per-request `requestPhase` and `elapsedMs` from the server and forwards them as `serverPhase` to the client. Focused maintained tests passed: testing:runs 23528128, 2 passed / 0 failed for the phase log and queue-vs-refresh timing cases.\n\n## Left\nA real installed-service integration test is running detached as 89578231-4ba5-4994-b690-ede3e5576df0 after the foreground 45s cap; check `testing:run-status`. If it passes, close as already-fixed/verified-existing with the original phase limitation explicit. If it fails, diagnose that exact result before closure.\n\n## Key insight\nThe original request reached a queued service state before the client deadline; a pre-existing drain was active when it arrived. The specific delay source cannot be assigned without server-side phase records. New current-source tracing gives that missing phase evidence on future requests; it does not prove or retroactively explain the old internal delay.\n\n## Next action\nCheck the detached integration result and current loop wake status; then finish the item only with evidence-backed closure.\n\n---\n## Did\nRoot cause at the client boundary: request ee58ed89-1bfc-4d97-bdb2-aaf86476f73f received a `queued` progress response, then reached the fixed 30000ms client deadline and fell back to the full compile. `typecheckViaService` routes that timeout through its fallback path. The service journal had no entries for 2026-11-05 16:10–16:14 local, so the exact internal server phase cannot be recovered. Current staging source emits per-request `requestPhase` and `elapsedMs` from the server and forwards `serverPhase` to the client.\n\nVerification: testing:runs 23528128 passed both focused phase-log and queue-vs-refresh timing tests (0 failed) at HEAD df01a886c5d27fa88c0f9fd65ac8f3e207e7eb6b. Detached installed-service integration run 89578231-4ba5-4994-b690-ede3e5576df0 completed with status pass and exitCode 0 on the same clean HEAD; it exercises the exact installed socket route without full fallback. No files were changed in this session.\n\n## Left\nClose this item as resolved with the unknown historical subphase explicitly recorded; the current trace and regression guard cover future requests. Then let the next loop wake call scheduler:get_next for the next admitted item.\n\n## Key insight\nThe queued response falsifies a never-connected/client-handshake explanation. It establishes acceptance into queued service work, but the old build did not report which phase consumed the 30s. New traces provide that distinction going forward; they do not retroactively identify the old phase.\n\n## Next action\nValidate the evidence-backed resolved close, refresh the cold carry, and close EI-27984456739598051."},"decidedReason":null,"finalR1Disposition":"OPEN"},{"index":50,"id":"EI-27984819582799065","title":"Managed task terminal await timed out despite an earlier successful prerequisite receipt","currentState":"done","sourceOriginal":"/mnt/data/papercusp-audit-inputs/c52-r1-frozen-20261106T1917Z/EI-27984819582799065.json","classification":"counterevidence-timeout-not-terminal","assessment":"Terminal capture occurred44.132s after await expiry, refuting that specific timeout expectation. Focused test command `npm run test:file -- packages/operator-core/lib/agent-tools/release/cut.test.ts` passed 98/98 at test provenance HEAD 0224e5c976.\n\n## Left\nThe code is not yet verified in a post-fix staging process. dev:pipeline_position at 17:56Z reported awaiting-push for cut.ts; staging-api was then wedged, but dev:service_health at 18:02Z later observed HTTP 200/17ms without a restart. No post-fix prepare-tag dry-run has been run. Existing issue EI-25370058683732162 tracks the staging listener/unaccepted-connection symptom; its root cause remains unresolved.\n\n## Key insight\nThe previous dry-run omitted root, so the implicit-root branch bypassed all measured phases. MCP logging itself was wired to the process logger; the missing phase was produced upstream by the early return.\n\n## Next action\nOn the next wake, re-read pipeline_position and service_health. If the change is on staging and the endpoint is healthy, restart with dev:restart only if needed and perform one prepare-tag dry-run with root omitted, then verify the path-free journal phase. The coordinated restart attempts timed out behind live shared staging leases and no restart completed; I released my exclusive drain.\nRan exactly one read-only :3170 prepare-tag dry-run with version 0.0.33, channel alpha, sourceSha 8af7f2d6b04ff9b1ad2b0504144136292ada0feb, no confirm and no root. It returned ok:true/dry_run:true, tag desktop-v0.0.33-alpha, remoteRefWrite:true (preview only), githubRelease:false, artifactUpload:false. The response includes the selected root/wouldRun path.\nThe dry-run’s path-free “prepare-tag root timing” event is NOT yet verified: logs:read for papercusp-staging-api.service with that phrase returned matched=0. A broader journal read showed the release:cut MCP request completed successfully, but no phase line. Prior focused tests remain as checkpointed: source-clone 12/12 and three targeted cut cases passed.\n\n## Left\nInspect the ToolContext.log sink/wiring for the staging release:cut handler and find the emitted phase record (possibly a non-journal tool log). If the call emitted no phase events, fix that class and add a regression guard before closing. Do not repeat the dry-run unless the first run’s evidence is reconciled. The earlier focused evidence remains: source-clone suite 12/12, then three targeted cut cases passed after fixture corrections.\nThe new prepare-tag path accepts version/channel/sourceSha. With confirm omitted and root omitted it is read-only and emits path-free “prepare-tag root timing” phases. I released my own exclusive draining lease; no restart occurred. :3170 health was up. A later leader said their drain was released, but I have not verified a successful runtime reload.\n\n## Left\nWait for the current staging shared lease/leader restart to finish. Re-read locks:list; only after no conflicting holder, use dev:restart target=staging with confirm/authorize and reason. Then verify the loaded marker “prepare-tag root timing” with dev:pipeline_position path cut.ts, testClass operator-api. Re-read staging release:cut status, then make one dry-run prepare-tag call with version 0.0.33, channel alpha, sourceSha 8af7f2d6b04ff9b1ad2b0504144136292ada0feb, omitting confirm and root. Record the path-free phase result and close only with verified evidence.\n\n## Key insight\nThe functional acceptance route is the committed origin/staging mirror on :3170; local test passes do not prove the staging handler loaded the change.\n\n---\n## Did\nImplemented path-free prepare-tag root-resolution phase logging and safe child-process git command/code/signal diagnostics in the release:cut source-clone verifier. Added coverage for handler wiring and for exit-code/SIGTERM failures without path leakage. The source-clone suite passed 12/12 in the first focused run; after correcting two stale cut.test fixtures, the three selected cases passed (95 filtered).\nThe failures were fixture drift: compact JSON no longer matches the version-only residue parser's line-oriented top-level field; invalid retry residue now reports root_resume_residue_invalid. No runtime root cause is established yet.\nPipeline routing says operator-api tests use the committed origin/staging mirror. :3170 is still on build 15e04a8c1a and dev:pipeline_position reported blockedOn=committed.
